Appendix 7.D
Local Lab Activities

I have also contributed to hardware activities for the Fleming group local lab. This started at Wright Laboratory at Yale, and local lab activities are also planned University of Chicago in the near future.

Any LArTPC requires careful monitoring of temperature, since there is a small
3.5^{\circ}
C range where argon remains liquid at atmospheric pressure. Additionally, it is desirable to have an independent system which can control heaters in order to maintain these temperatures, even in the event of a computer failure. For this reason, I developed a 16 channel Resistive Temperature Device (RTD) reader as an Arduino shield, as shown in Fig.
11
. I make use of multiplexing and analog amplification in order to accurately and precisely measure temperatures from 16 separate PT-1000 resistive temperature sensors. In addition to communicating with a computer, the Arduino can also run its own simple logic in order to control a heater attached to the condenser of a pulse tube refrigerator. This printed circuit board which I designed, constructed, and tested is very cheap, and is potentially able to replace many expensive specialty commercial PT-1000 readers, especially when many readout channels are required. This design can potentially be applicable to a wide variety of applications, and it is available at
https://github.com/leehagaman/rtd-reader
.

(a)

(b)

Figure 11
:
16 Channel RTD reader. Panel (a) shows the design showing each two layer connections. Panel (b) shows a model of the PCB as it appears after printing.

Chapter 8
Wire-Cell BDT Variables

This appendix describes Wire-Cell reconstruction variables used for
\nu_{e}
,
\nu_{\mu}
, and
\pi^{0}
selections described in Chapter
2
, as well as for the Wire-Cell
\pi^{0}
and NC Delta radiative selections described in Chapter
3
.

These variables are grouped into sets, originally for specific “taggers”, which were each designed to reject a certain type of event using human-designed cuts. This causes some information to be duplicated, with the same variable existing with different names corresponding to different taggers. Some of these variables are “vector variables”, and contain more than one entry per event, for example variables related to each shower in an event. Some of these vector variables are vectors for historical reasons, and actually contain only global information about the neutrino interaction, or always contain only one entry. Some of the variables developed for these taggers were not used for the final BDT input, and those variables are not included here.

Many of these variables are related to the “primary shower”, meaning the highest energy shower connected to the reconstructed neutrino vertex, which will be the electron shower in correctly reconstructed
\nu_{e}
CC events. Some of these variables refer to “attached” segments, which directly touch other activity, or “connected” segments, which are connected in the same cluster but perhaps with other segments in between.

All of these variables refer only to reconstructed quantities, and are applied in the same way to data and simulation. Note that these are simplified descriptions, and there are many edge cases, kinematic thresholds, and candidate reconstructions with different confidence levels involved in these variable definitions; these details can only be fully described by directly analyzing the code that we reference in each table below.

Variable

Description

gap_flag

Indicates if a significant gap is present in the shower.

gap_flag_prolong_u

Indicates that the shower has significant extent in the U wire plane.

gap_flag_prolong_v

Indicates that the shower has significant extent in the V wire plane.

gap_flag_prolong_w

Indicates that the shower has significant extent in the W (also called Z) wire plane.

gap_flag_parallel

Indicates that the shower is nearly parallel to the wire planes (isochronous activity).

gap_n_points

Total number of evaluated 3D shower points.

gap_n_bad

Total number of evaluated 3D shower points with missing information.

gap_energy

Shower energy in MeV.

gap_num_valid_tracks

Number of tracks connected to the neutrino vertex.

gap_flag_single_shower

Indicates if the shower is the only activity at the reconstruced neutrino vertex.

gap_filled

Flag indicating if the information in the above variables has been filled for this event.

Table 1
:
Description of
\nu_{e}
CC inclusive selection variables related to shower gap detection. The full code defining these is available at
https://github.com/BNLIF/wire-cell-pid/blob/master/src/NeutrinoID_nue_tagger.h

Description

kine_reco_add_energy

Additional energy added to the total reconstructed neutrino energy, including 8.6 MeV per proton and the particle mass for each pion, muon, and electron.

Reconstructed invariant mass of two showers from a
\pi^{0}
using the shower energies
E_{1}
,
E_{2}
and the opening angle between the two
\theta
using the formula
m=\sqrt{4E_{1}E_{2}\sin^{2}\theta/2}
.

kine_pio_flag

This value is 0 if a
\pi^{0}
is not reconstructed, 1 if a
\pi^{0}
is identified with visible activity at the neutrino vertex, and 2 if a
\pi^{0}
is identified with no visible activity at the neutrino vertex.

Distance between the
\pi^{0}
vertex and the neutrino vertex.

Energy of the first
\pi^{0}
photon shower in MeV.

Angle with respect to the beam for the first
\pi^{0}
photon shower in degrees.

Azimuthal angle with respect to the beam for the first
\pi^{0}
photon shower in degrees.

\pi^{0}
vertex and the first photon shower vertex in MeV.

Energy of the second
\pi^{0}
photon shower in MeV.

\pi^{0}

\nu_{\mu}
CC variables. Description of
\nu_{\mu}
CC inclusive selection variables related to the “cosmic five” tagger. The full code defining these is available at
https://github.com/BNLIF/wire-cell-pid/blob/master/src/NeutrinoID_cosmic_tagger.h
.

Variable

Description

cosmict_6_filled

Indicates if COSMICT6 variables have been filled.

cosmict_6_flag_dir_weak

Indicates if the 2nd longest muon candidate track has uncertain direction reconstruction.

cosmict_6_flag_inside

Indicates if the 2nd longest muon candidate track endpoint is contained within the fiducial volume.

cosmict_6_angle

Angle between the 2nd longest candidate muon track direction and the longest candidate muon track direction in degrees.

cosmict_flag_6

Indicates if the above variables indicate the likely presence of the two candidate muon tracks coming from a single cosmic ray muon track.

Variable

Description

cosmict_7_filled

Indicates if COSMICT7 variables have been filled.

cosmict_7_flag_sec

Indicates if a 2nd longest candidate muon track is likely to be back-to-back with the longest candidate muon track.

cosmict_7_n_muon_tracks

Number of daughter tracks from the candidate muon.

cosmict_7_total_shower_length

Summed length of the daughter tracks from the candidate muon in cm.

cosmict_7_flag_inside

Indicates if the candidate muon track is contained within the fiducial volume.

cosmict_7_angle_beam

Angle between the candidate muon track direction and the beam direction in degrees.

cosmict_7_flag_dir_weak

Indicates if the candidate muon track has uncertain direction reconstruction.

cosmict_7_dQ_dx_end

dQ/dx
at the end of the candidate muon track.

cosmict_7_dQ_dx_front

dQ/dx
at the start of the candidate muon track.

cosmict_7_theta

Polar angle of the candidate muon with respect to the beam direction in degrees.

cosmict_7_phi

Azimuthal angle of the candidate muon with respect to the beam direction in degrees.

cosmict_flag_7

Indicates if the above variables indicate the likely presence of a cosmic ray muon track.

Description

cosmict_8_flag_out

Variable

Description

cosmict_10_vtx_z

Vector variable of the z coordinate of the endpoint of each segment attached to the neutrino vertex in cm.

cosmict_10_flag_shower

Vector variable indicating if each segment attached to the neutrino vertex is identified as part of a shower.

cosmict_10_flag_dir_weak

Vector variable indicating if each segment attached to the neutrino vertex has uncertain direction information.

cosmict_10_angle_beam

Vector variable of the angle between the beam direction and the direction of each segment attached to the neutrino vertex.

cosmict_10_length

Vector variable of the length of each segment attached to the neutrino vertex in cm.

cosmict_10_score

Minimum score of the
numu_2
BDT across all entries in the vector.

:
COSMICT10
\nu_{\mu}
CC variables. Description of
\nu_{\mu}

Variable

Description

cosmict_flag_1

Indicates if the neutrino vertex is outside the fiducial volume.

cosmict_flag_9

Indicates if an event is likely to have a cosmic ray origin using topological, geometric, and energy-based information.

cosmic_filled

Indicates if cosmic ray tagging variables have been filled.

cosmic_flag

Indicates if an event was initially suspected as a cosmic ray, but then this decision was reversed after certain features indicated that it is potentially a
\nu_{e}
CC candidate.

cosmict_flag

Indicates if any of
cosmict_flag_1
,
cosmict_flag_2
,
cosmict_flag_3
,
cosmict_flag_4
,
cosmict_flag_5
,
cosmict_flag_6
,
cosmict_flag_7
,
cosmict_flag_8
, or
cosmict_flag_9
are true, indicating a likely cosmic origin for the event.

numu_cc_flag

A value of -1 indicates that an event has failed Wire-Cell generic neutrino selection, a value of 0 indicates that an event has failed the cut-based Wire-Cell
\nu_{\mu}
CC selection, and a value of 1 indicates that an event has passed the cut-based Wire-Cell
\nu_{\mu}
CC selection.

:

and
